They come from 9 LLM families (10 few-shot slots), 2 zero-shot variants, the frontier model\n  GPT-5.1 on 200 sentences, ccg2lambda, and the MALLS GPT-4 gold treated as a system.\n- Each row is labelled by the shared solver labeller (`labeller/`, the ⊕-precedence-fixed iter-3 code) and by a blind,\n  disguised, family-disjoint LLM panel: Haiku-4.5 / GLM-4.6 / Kimi-K2. The panel passed a known-label gate, and its\n  accuracy on real errors is measured against expert corrections.\n- The panel also audits every reference, and wrong MALLS references were repaired by panel agreement where possible.\n- The rebuilt shared screen (Logic-LM track L + curated track H) is included, keyed by the screen's exact `item_id`.\n\n**Read `dataset_card.md` first.** It holds every data-quality statistic, the label rule verbatim, the panel calibration and\nthe known biases.\n\n## Headline numbers\n- Final labels (held-out rows): `{\"UNPARSEABLE\": 1086, \"CORRECT\": 1750, \"ERROR\": 5005, \"UNRESOLVED\": 276, \"CONTESTED\": 390}`. Tiers: `{\"-\": 1086, \"A\": 1044, \"B\": 2157, \"C\": 3891, \"none\": 276, \"A_unaudited_ref\": 53}`.\n- Testability (LLM rows, tiers A+B, CONTESTED and reading_choice excluded; each cell is rows (distinct sentences)):\n\n| stratum | CORRECT A+B | ERROR A+B | testable | CORRECT tier A | ERROR tier A |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| L25 | 176 (78) | 697 (107) | yes | 40 (25) | 98 (46) |\n| L25_orig200 | 141 (54) | 567 (70) | yes | 34 (19) | 73 (30) |\n| L25_topup100 | 35 (24) | 130 (37) | no | 6 (6) | 25 (16) |\n| L20 | 229 (65) | 592 (79) | yes | 64 (33) | 132 (51) |\n| EXC | 222 (55) | 384 (63) | yes | 124 (38) | 129 (48) |\n| CTRL | 237 (31) | 149 (28) | yes | 186 (20) | 94 (15) |\n\n- Panel: its majority accuracy on the 75 unambiguous expert-corrected real-error pairs is 0.727. It\n  accepts only 0.613 of the expert-corrected formulas, so it is STRICT.\n  - The reference audit rejects 440/535 MALLS golds and\n    112/148 CTRL references. Part of this is likely panel over-strictness\n    (card §0 and §7).\n  - Cohen κ between GLM and Kimi is 0.5133; the\n    3-rater Fleiss κ on the pilot is 0.5391.\n- Solver labeller vs panel majority: error precision 0.7879, error recall\n  0.8363.\n- OpenRouter spend: $9.833, still under the $10 artifact budget. The plan cap was $9.50; it was raised to\n  $9.80 for the last repair and adjudication calls, and one batch overshot. See card §0.\n\n## Deliverables\n| Path | What |\n|---|---|\n| `full_data_out.json` | **THE DATASET** (aii-json `exp_sel_data_out`, validated; 27.7 MB, one file). Groups: `heldout_candidates` (8,507), `heldout_sentences` (700), `panel_calibration` (173), `screen_audit` (1,173). Absolute path: `/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/full_data_out.json` |\n| `mini_data_out.json`, `preview_data_out.json` | First 3 rows per group; the preview truncates strings to 200 chars |\n| `screen_adjudicated_labels.json` | `{item_id: {track, system, auto_label, repair_ops, panel_votes, final_label, label_tier, reading_choice, join_keys, ...}}` for the screen. Absolute path: `/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/screen_adjudicated_labels.json` |\n| `prereg_strata.json` | Strata definitions and exclusion counts (frozen at selection), the L25 top-up execution record, and the testability declaration (`testability_declaration.primary_pool`, made before any metric run) |\n| `dataset_card.md` | Data-quality statistics, the label rule, gate and real-error tables, deviations, licences |\n| `work/label_report.json` | Every statistic in the card, machine-readable |\n| `cost_ledger.jsonl` | One line per OpenRouter call: phase, model, tokens, usage.cost |\n| `data.py` | `uv run data.py` builds `full_data_out.json` from `work/assembled.json` and re-verifies EVERY row against the sources in `temp/datasets/`. Result: `metadata_source_verified` is true for 10,553/10,553 rows |\n\nRow format:\n- `input` is a JSON string `{text, candidate_fol, reference_fol, system, prompt_variant}`.\n- `output` is the final label: `CORRECT` / `ERROR` / `CONTESTED` / `UNRESOLVED` / `UNPARSEABLE`. Sentence rows carry the\n  reference status instead.\n\nKey `metadata_*` fields:\n- identity: `item_id` (sha1(system|norm(text)|raw_output)[:16], the screen recipe), `sentence_id`, `system`, `slot`,\n  `family`, `system_class`, `prompt_variant`, `raw_output`, `normalisation_applied`;\n- solver: `auto_label`, `equiv_status`, `repair_ops`, `repair_status`, `convention_flags`;\n- panel: `panel_votes` (per model: faithful, ops, conf), `panel_item`, `panel_ambiguous`, `panel_scope`;\n- final label: `error_ops`, `label_tier` (A / B / C / A_unaudited_ref / none / -), `label_source`, `correct_not_equivalent`,\n  `reading_choice`, `addrop_only_suspect`;\n- reference: `gold_audit_flag`, `reference_status`;\n- `strata` {words, n_quant, depth, n_conditions, text_conditions, exception_type, source_stratum, ctrl_len_bin,\n  l25_topup_batch};\n- `disguised_text`, `disguised_fol`, `class_id`, `class_size`, `cost_usd`.\n\n**Primary analysis**: tiers A+B, excluding CONTESTED and reading_choice rows. Bootstraps must resample by `sentence_id`.\n**Robustness**: tier A only. **Secondary**: tier C (panel-only labels for sentences without a trusted reference).\n\n## Layout\n- `labeller/`: the shared labeller.\n  - `fol.py`: the iter-3 parser with the ⊕-precedence and `<->` fixes.\n  - `repair_census.py`.\n  - `label_lib.py`: `equivalent_modulo_vocab`, `minimal_typed_repair`, `convention_flags`, `auto_label`.\n  - `disguise.py`: the nonce disguise.\n  - `complexity_counts.py`, `lint_smells.py`: reference copies; L1 lint is NOT run.\n- `src/`: pipeline scripts.\n  - `select_sentences.py`, `select_topup.py`: sentence selection and the L25 top-up.\n  - `generate.py`, `ccg2lambda_candidates.py`, `normalise.py`: candidate generation and normalisation.\n  - `screen.py`: the screen rebuild.\n  - `run_label.py`, `label_worker.py`, `extend_labels.py`: solver labelling; `extend_labels.py` adds new rows without\n    renumbering classes.\n  - `calibration.py`, `gate.py`: the gate and track H.\n  - `panel.py`, `panel_run.py`: the panel, including the `--adjudicate`, `--ctrl-reduced` and `--vg-only` modes.\n  - `screen_scope.py`, `repair_refs.py`.\n  - `assemble.py`, `stats.py`, `card.py`: the final label rule, the statistics and the card.\n  - `or_client.py`: an async OpenRouter client with a cost ledger and budget stops.\n  - `resume_panel.sh`: the LLM steps in the order they were executed.\n- `tests/test_fol.py`: parser regression tests.\n- `prompts/`: `fewshot_v1.txt` (the frozen generation prompt) and `zeroshot_v1.txt`.\n- `raw/` (irreproducible API outputs, keep):\n  - `generations.jsonl`: all generator outputs; the later of duplicate keys wins;\n  - `reference_repair.jsonl`: the panel-written references;\n  - `ccg2lambda_candidates.jsonl`;\n  - `logiclm/`;\n  - `hf/`: HF downloads.\n- `work/`:\n  - `sentences.json` (700 rows), `sentences_topup.json`, `sentences_600_before_topup.json`;\n  - `labels_heldout.jsonl`, `labels_heldout_repaired.jsonl`, `labels_screen.jsonl`: per-sentence classes and labels;\n  - `panel_cache.jsonl`: every panel response;\n  - `panel_heldout.jsonl` and `panel_heldout_adj.jsonl`: stage 1, and the Haiku adjudication;\n  - `panel_screen.jsonl`, `panel_screen_vg.jsonl`;\n  - `reference_overrides.json`, `no_trusted_reference.json`;\n  - `gate_results.json` (synthetic gate + track H), `trackh_panel_rows.json`;\n  - `assembled.json`, `label_report.json`;\n  - backups of the pre-panel versions: `*_v1_blocked.py.txt`, `labels_heldout.jsonl.bak_before_extend`.\n- `data_local/`: read-only copies of the iter-3 source files (MALLS-v0.1, folio-refined, DSAVlab curated).\n- `temp/datasets/`: symlinks to every source used by `data.py`. No external dataset search was needed: the plan fixes all\n  sources.\n\n## How to rerun (each step is resumable; the panel cache never re-bills)\n```\nuv venv .venv --python=3.12 && uv pip install --python=.venv/bin/python -r pyproject.toml\n.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -c pytest.ini tests/test_fol.py                   # step 0b regression\n.venv/bin/python src/select_sentences.py                                        # step 1 (the 6 exemplars were hand-picked, card §10)\n.venv/bin/python src/generate.py --limit 12 && .venv/bin/python src/generate.py --cap 3.5   # step 2\n.venv/bin/python src/ccg2lambda_candidates.py && .venv/bin/python src/screen.py             # step 2d, 6a\n.venv/bin/python src/run_label.py --kind heldout --budget 8 && .venv/bin/python src/run_label.py --kind screen --budget 8   # step 3\n.venv/bin/python src/calibration.py && .venv/bin/python src/gate.py --mode synthetic --members P1,P2,P3,R1 --cap 0.45       # step 4a/4b\nbash src/resume_panel.sh    # 4c, top-up, 5b-5e, 6b, then assemble -> stats -> card -> data.py\n```\nOpenRouter needs `OPENROUTER_API_KEY`. `AII_HARD_CAP` (default 9.50) is the cumulative-spend stop.\n\n## Restoring removed files\n- `.venv/` (regenerable): `uv venv .venv --python=3.12 && uv pip install --python=.venv/bin/python -r pyproject.toml`\n- `nltk_data/` (redownloadable):\n  `.venv/bin/python -c \"import nltk; [nltk.download(p, download_dir='nltk_data') for p in ('wordnet','omw-1.4')]\"`\n- `raw/hf/` (redownloadable):\n  `.venv/bin/python -c \"from huggingface_hub import hf_hub_download as d; [d('tasksource/folio', f, repo_type='dataset', local_dir='raw/hf/tasksource__folio') for f in ('folio_v2_train.jsonl','folio_v2_validation.jsonl')]; [d('kenken6696/folio_by_ccg2lambda', f, repo_type='dataset', local_dir='raw/hf/kenken6696__folio_by_ccg2lambda') for f in ('data/train-00000-of-00001.parquet','data/valid-00000-of-00001.parquet')]\"`.\n  The `temp/datasets/` symlinks point into it.\n- `__pycache__/`, `src/__pycache__/`, `tests/__pycache__/`, `labeller/__pycache__/` (regenerable): Python bytecode,\n  recreated on import.\n- `.pytest_cache/` (regenerable): `.venv/bin/python -m pytest -q -c pytest.ini tests/test_fol.py`\n", "numLines": 142, "startLine": 1, "totalLines": 142}}

{"type": "text", "file": {"filePath": "/ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1/dataset_card.md", "content": "# Dataset card: held-out NL→FOL faithfulness meta-evaluation set (run_u75jRHUss0zo, invention iter 1, dataset E)\n\n> **HELD-OUT: iteration 2 must not tune any threshold on these rows.** Use them ONCE, to confirm a screen survivor whose\n> thresholds are already frozen. Tune and select on the screen (the `screen_audit` group / `screen_adjudicated_labels.json`).\n\n**Status: COMPLETE.** Generation, solver labelling, the blind 3-family panel pass (gold audit + adjudication), the\nreference repair, the track-H real-error check and the screen audit have all run.\n\n**History.** A first pass (2026-09-23, morning UTC) was cut off at 13:29 UTC when other runs exhausted the shared OpenRouter\nkey's $50/day limit. That release had solver-only labels and 5,128 UNRESOLVED rows. After the key reset, the pass resumed\nfrom its caches. On resumption:\n- the 541 Qwen zero-shot generations lost to the limit were regenerated;\n- the pre-registered L25 top-up was executed;\n- the panel ran.\n\nTotals: 700 sentences, 8507 candidate rows,\n173 calibration rows, 1173 screen rows. Total OpenRouter cost\n**$9.833** (hard cap $9.50).\n\nHeld-out final labels: `{\"UNPARSEABLE\": 1086, \"CORRECT\": 1750, \"ERROR\": 5005, \"UNRESOLVED\": 276, \"CONTESTED\": 390}`. Tiers: `{\"-\": 1086, \"A\": 1044, \"B\": 2157, \"C\": 3891, \"none\": 276, \"A_unaudited_ref\": 53}`.\n\n### Headline data-quality facts (read before using the labels)\n1. **Testability (tiers A+B, primary pool).** The testable strata are L25 (all 300), L20, EXC and CTRL. The L25 top-up batch\n   alone is not testable, and is meant to be pooled. See section 3.\n2. **The panel is strict, and the reference audit rejects most references.**\n   - It judged 440/535 MALLS gold formulas unfaithful\n     (0.822 [0.788, 0.853]) and 112/148 CTRL\n     references (0.757 [0.682, 0.819]). Expert audits report about 36-46%.\n   - On track H the same panel accepts only 0.613\n     of expert-CORRECTED formulas.\n   - So a large part of these rejections is probably panel over-strictness (false alarms on long, multi-condition formulas),\n     not reference error. Tier-C rows (unrepaired or disputed references) and all panel-decided ERROR labels inherit this\n     bias. The panel's faithful rate over all shown items is only about 0.2 (section 7).\n   - The label ERROR therefore means \"at least 2 of 3 small judges found a meaning difference\", and should be read that way.\n3. **Tier A vs tier B.**\n   - Tier A (solver-decided against an audited or repaired reference) is the most panel-independent layer. Iteration 2\n     should confirm on tier A alone as a robustness check. Tier-A counts are printed in section 3.\n   - Tier B (panel-decided for VOCAB_GRAN/COMPOUND/TIMEOUT) has majority accuracy of about 0.73 on real errors.\n4. **Budget deviations.**\n   - The plan's cap was $9.50. It was raised to $9.80 for the final reference-repair tie-breaks and the adjudication\n     remainder. The last batch of 16 in-flight calls overshot, so the final spend is $9.833, still under\n     the $10 artifact budget.\n   - The adjudication remainder was not finished for 32 low-priority sentences. Their split items are CONTESTED (a 1-1\n     split with a missing vote) or UNRESOLVED (a single vote).\n   - 120 stage-1 sentences lost their Kimi call to the phase cap. Haiku then judged those items, so they have two votes,\n     and any 1-1 split is CONTESTED.\n5. **CTRL UNRESOLVED rows.** They arise where a CTRL reference was DISPUTED (tier C → panel-only labels) but the sentence\n   was outside the 20% full-coverage sample. Its auto-ERROR classes were never shown to the panel, so they stay UNRESOLVED\n   rather than taking a solver label measured against a disputed reference.\n\n## 1. Composition\n- **heldout_sentences** (700), all screen-disjoint (hash collisions after exclusion:\n  0):\n  - **L25 = 300**: MALLS-v0.1-train, ≥25 words AND ≥3 gold-derived conditions, sha1 order, spread over word bins 25-29 / 30-34\n    / ≥35.\n    - 200 were selected in step 1.\n    - The other 100 are the **pre-registered top-up** batch: the same rule on the unused pool, bins taken\n      [43, 42, 15]. The ≥35-word supply was exhausted at 15.\n    - The top-up rule was frozen in `prereg_strata.json` before any label was seen: \"If after Step 3 the tier-A-projected\n      ERROR or CORRECT count of L25 is below 60, generate one more sha1-ordered batch of 100 L25 sentences with the 5 cheapest\n      generator slots\". Tier-A CORRECT was 9. The top-up was generated with the 5 cheapest slots (Command-R7B, Llama-3.1-8B,\n      Phi-4, Mistral-Small-3.2, Qwen3-235B) plus the MALLS gold, so its rows per sentence differ from the original 200.\n    - `metadata_strata.l25_topup_batch` marks these rows. Statistics are also reported with and without the top-up.\n  - **L20 = 150**: 20-24 words and ≥3 conditions.\n  - **EXC = 100**: all 67 parseable unless/except/excluding/other-than items plus 33 'without' items with ≥15 words. The XOR\n    pattern 'but not (both)' is excluded.\n  - **CTRL = 150** FOLIO-v2-train premises where the tasksource original ≡ folio-refined modulo vocabulary. Bins are\n    50/60/40 by length <12 / 12-19 / ≥20 words. Reference = the refined formula. agreement_type: `{\"IDENTICAL_STRING\": 113, \"EQ\": 24, \"VOCAB\": 13}`.\n    IDENTICAL_STRING shows only that the refiner did not change the formula; it is weak evidence. The blind panel audit of\n    the reference (section 4) is the real check.\n- **heldout_candidates** (8507 rows):\n  - 10 LLM slots over 9 families, one fixed few-shot prompt (`prompts/fewshot_v1.txt`), temperature 0: Llama-3.1-8B,\n    Llama-3.3-70B, Qwen3-235B-A22B-2507, Mistral-Small-3.2-24B, DeepSeek-V3.2 (reasoning off), Gemma-3-27B, Phi-4,\n    GPT-4.1-mini, Gemini-2.5-Flash (reasoning off), Command-R7B;\n  - zero-shot variants for 2 families: Llama-3.3-70B and Qwen3-235B;\n  - frontier GPT-5.1 (effort low) on a 200-sentence subset (60 L25 + 50 L20 + 40 EXC + 50 CTRL);\n  - ccg2lambda (system_class `symbolic_eventsem`): 57 CTRL sentences matched in the HF train\n    split. Too few to analyse on its own, and never pooled with LLM rows;\n  - the MALLS GPT-4 gold itself, as system `malls_gpt4_gold` (system_class `reference_gold_as_system`), on every MALLS\n    sentence. Its label is the blind gold audit.\n  - Every unparseable output is kept (final label UNPARSEABLE).\n- **panel_calibration**: the synthetic known-label gate (77 items) and the 96 track-H human real-error pairs with panel\n  votes.\n- **screen_audit** (1173 rows): track L = Logic-LM's released FOLIO-dev outputs; track H = the 302\n  curated FOLIO/MALLS items. They are keyed by the screen's exact `item_id`, and the same labels are in\n  `screen_adjudicated_labels.json`.\n\n## 2. Final label rule (plan 5f, verbatim; `src/assemble.py::final_rule`)\n- auto CORRECT (plain z3 EQ to the reference) → final CORRECT, tier A. Panel dissent is logged as panel false-alarm evidence.\n- auto ERROR(ops) → final ERROR(ops), tier A, unless ≥2/3 panel models say faithful → CONTESTED (a likely convention,\n  aligner or reference issue; convention_flags are recorded).\n- auto VOCAB_GRAN → panel majority: faithful → CORRECT (correct_not_equivalent=true, tier B); unfaithful →\n  ERROR('MEANING_RENAME' + panel ops, tier B).\n- auto COMPOUND or TIMEOUT_UNKNOWN → panel majority: unfaithful → ERROR('COMPOUND', panel ops, tier B); faithful → CORRECT\n  (correct_not_equivalent=true, tier B).\n- If the panel majority flags the sentence AMBIGUOUS, add reading_choice=true and keep it out of primary pooling, as a\n  separate class.\n- A missing vote (a model failed) with a 1-1 split → CONTESTED.\n- UNPARSEABLE → final UNPARSEABLE, kept in the denominator.\n- NO_TRUSTED_REFERENCE or DISPUTED_REFERENCE → final = panel majority, tier C (secondary only).\n\nPrimary analyses use tiers A+B, exclude CONTESTED (and reading_choice rows), and report sensitivity with CONTESTED counted\nas CORRECT and as ERROR.\n\n**Implementation details that matter for users:**\n1. **Class collapse.** Per sentence, candidates are union-found into classes modulo vocabulary (`equivalent_modulo_vocab`).\n   The panel judges one representative per class (disguised, blind, shuffled), and the label propagates to the members.\n   - The reference class c0 is shown unlabelled among the candidates, so the gold audit is blind.\n   - A candidate that joined c0 only modulo vocabulary or granularity (auto VOCAB_GRAN) is shown to the panel **as its own\n     item** (`metadata_panel_item` = `<sid>:vg:<hash>`). A vote on the reference itself cannot tell whether the renaming\n     preserved meaning.\n2. **Panel design (budget-driven, label-preserving).** GLM-4.6 (P3) and Kimi-K2-0905 (R1) judge every shown item. Haiku-4.5\n   (P1) judges only the items where they disagree or a vote is missing, plus the reference item when only the\n   sentence-level ambiguity flag is split. Because P1 is only needed when the other two disagree, the 2-of-3 majority is the\n   same as a full 3-vote majority. What is lost is a 3-rater κ on all items; it is reported on the 30-sentence pilot, which\n   all three judged.\n   - Why: the full 3-model pass was projected at $7.6 against about $6.3 of remaining budget.\n   - Adjudicator calls see fewer items than a full call (a mild format difference from the gate).\n3. **CTRL scope (plan 5c fallback).** For CTRL, a 20% sha1 sample of sentences gets full class coverage. For the rest, the\n   panel sees the reference class plus all VOCAB_GRAN/COMPOUND/TIMEOUT classes. Their auto-ERROR classes keep the solver\n   label (tier A, `label_source=solver`, no CONTESTED routing). MALLS strata (L25/L20/EXC) have full coverage.\n   `metadata_panel_scope` records which applies.\n4. **Reference audit and repair.**\n   - MALLS gold with ≥2 unfaithful votes = GOLD_WRONG.\n   - The panel models then write their own FOL for the original, undisguised sentence with the few-shot generation prompt.\n     GLM and Kimi go first; Haiku is called only when those two are not mutually equivalent modulo vocabulary. The raw\n     outputs are in `raw/reference_repair.jsonl`.\n   - If ≥2 formulas are mutually equivalent modulo vocabulary, the one with the fewest atoms becomes the reference\n     (PANEL_REPAIRED), and all rows are re-labelled by the solver against it (`work/labels_heldout_repaired.jsonl`).\n     Otherwise the status is NO_TRUSTED_REFERENCE (tier C).\n   - Caveat: a panel-written reference comes from the same families that judge the rows.\n   - CTRL references judged unfaithful are DISPUTED_REFERENCE (tier C).\n   - Panel votes on candidate classes are reference-free, so they stay valid after a repair.\n\n## 3. Label counts and the testability declaration\nCells are rows (distinct sentences), LLM systems only. The rule: a stratum is testable if it has ≥50 ERROR rows AND ≥50\nCORRECT rows AND ≥25 distinct sentences on each side, tier A+B, CONTESTED and reading_choice rows excluded. It was declared\nafter labelling and before any metric run, and is copied into `prereg_strata.json` (`testability_declaration.primary_pool`).\n\n| stratum | sents | LLM rows | CORRECT A+B | ERROR A+B | TESTABLE | CORRECT tier A | ERROR tier A | tier-A testable | CONTESTED | reading_choice | tier C | UNRESOLVED |\n|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|\n| L25 (all 300) | 300 | 2960 | 176 (78) | 697 (107) | **YES** | 40 (25) | 98 (46) | no | 130 | 5 | 1566 | 4 |\n|   L25 original 200 | 200 | 2460 | 141 (54) | 567 (70) | **YES** | 34 (19) | 73 (30) | no | 116 | 0 | 1334 | 4 |\n|   L25 top-up 100 | 100 | 500 | 35 (24) | 130 (37) | no | 6 (6) | 25 (16) | no | 14 | 5 | 232 | 0 |\n| L20 | 150 | 1850 | 229 (65) | 592 (79) | **YES** | 64 (33) | 132 (51) | yes | 91 | 22 | 746 | 0 |\n| EXC | 100 | 1240 | 222 (55) | 384 (63) | **YES** | 124 (38) | 129 (48) | yes | 90 | 13 | 401 | 11 |\n| CTRL | 150 | 1850 | 237 (31) | 149 (28) | **YES** | 186 (20) | 94 (15) | no | 65 | 0 | 865 | 260 |\n\n**Tier-A-only** counts are printed next to A+B, so that iteration 2 can confirm on the panel-independent labels alone as a\nrobustness check. Tier-A CORRECT is structurally rare on MALLS strata: plain z3 EQ needs the reference's own predicate\nnames. EXC by exception type: `{\"except\": {\"CORRECT\": 80, \"UNPARSEABLE\": 44, \"ERROR\": 122, \"CONTESTED\": 20}, \"excluding\": {\"CORRECT\": 10, \"ERROR\": 16, \"CONTESTED\": 1, \"UNPARSEABLE\": 10}, \"unless\": {\"ERROR\": 291, \"UNPARSEABLE\": 53, \"CORRECT\": 124, \"CONTESTED\": 36, \"UNRESOLVED\": 10}, \"without\": {\"CORRECT\": 86, \"ERROR\": 279, \"CONTESTED\": 33, \"UNPARSEABLE\": 24, \"UNRESOLVED\": 1}}`. Tier-B/C labels carry panel error: about 0.73 accuracy on real errors, and the panel is strict (section 7).\n  2. A MALLS reference judged faithful may still be wrong if 2 of the 3 panel members missed the error.\n  3. CTRL references are FOLIO annotations the refiner left unchanged (mostly IDENTICAL_STRING); they are not independent\n     re-annotations.\n  4. Tier A depends on the iter-3 aligner. Its VOCAB/GRAN bridge can accept meaning-changing renames, which is why\n     VOCAB_GRAN rows go to the panel.\n  5. One prompt per generator at temperature 0, so system-level rankings are descriptive only.\n  6. The L25 top-up batch has only 5 generator slots.\n  7. SETUP (about 20 min, $0).\n- Copy E_DIR/labeller/, the E_DIR/src files {select_sentences.py, normalise.py, or_client.py, generate.py}, E_DIR/prompts/, E_DIR/tests/test_fol.py and E_DIR/pyproject.toml into the workspace (labeller/, src_e/, prompts/, tests/).\n- Create a venv with uv; add spacy with en_core_web_sm, nltk wordnet, and z3-solver (already a dependency of E).\n- Run pytest tests/test_fol.py. It must pass unchanged.\n- Verify sha1(prompts/fewshot_v1.txt) equals the prompt_sha1 in E's raw/generations.jsonl records.\n- Set AII_HARD_CAP=9.5 in or_client, and give each phase a cap (see the BUDGET table).\n- Snapshot the OpenRouter model list and confirm that every slot id in generate.py SLOTS exists. If one 404s, use the nearest same-family successor and log the substitution in generation_manifest.json. Never change a family.\n\nSTEP 1. SOURCE ATOM POOL (about 40 min, $0).\nBuild source_rules.json from, in priority order:\n  (a) E heldout_sentences with reference_status TRUSTED_AGREED (36, FOLIO CTRL) or GOLD_PANEL_OK (95, MALLS);\n  (b) ctrl_pool() output: FOLIO-v2-train premises with agreement_type in {IDENTICAL_STRING, EQ, VOCAB} (about 1,500), minus E's 6 few-shot exemplars;\n  (c) FALLBACK, only if fewer than 150 audited lexicon entries survive STEP 2: MALLS-train sentences of 15 words or fewer, shaped as below, sha1 order.\nApply the exclusion hashes from build_exclusion() to every source text; screen collisions must be 0.\nFrom each source formula, extract only atoms over the rule's single universally quantified variable x:\n- unary P(x), or binary P(x, const) / P(const, x) with a lowercase constant;\n- no atoms with other bound variables.\nRecord for each atom: role (restrictor, consequent or other), sortal flag, and the source sentence and formula.\nPrefer rules shaped as forall x (L1 [and L2 [and L3]] -> L), where each L is a literal over x.\n\nSTEP 2. ATOM LEXICON: extraction, deterministic checks, audit (about 50 min, ≤$0.8).\n2a. Extraction. anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5 (an Anthropic model; neither a generator nor a metric-judge family), 5 source rules per call, temperature 0. Input: the sentence, the formula and the atom list. Output JSON per atom:\n  {vp_sg_pos (3rd-person-singular verb phrase for 'the person ___', e.g. 'is a student', 'lives in Paris', 'can make cookies'), vp_sg_neg ('is not a student'), noun (only for sortal atoms, e.g. 'student'), subject_sort (person / animal / object / place / organisation / other), or SKIP if no faithful phrase exists}.\n  Cap about 320 source rules (≈64 calls).\n2b. Deterministic checks, reject on failure:\n  - every content-word Porter stem of vp_sg_pos appears in the source sentence stems, or is a WordNet synonym of one (auxiliaries, articles and 'not' are exempt);\n  - the first token of vp_sg_pos is tagged VBZ or MD by spaCy, or is is/has/does;\n  - vp_sg_neg contains not / n't / no / never;\n  - the constant in a binary atom appears in the phrase (case-insensitive, camelCase split).\n2c. Sonnet audit. The adjudicator model (default anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6, or the sibling R_ADJ's gated model), 20 entries per call. It sees each (source sentence, formula, atom, vp_pos, vp_neg) and returns OK / WRONG plus a reason code.\n2d. Keep the OK entries and write lexicon.json, with lexicon_id = sha1(atom|vp)[:10].\n2e. Resolve name collisions across sources. If two entries share a predicate name but differ in arity or in phrase meaning, rename the later one to Name_k and record the change.\n2f. Group entries by subject_sort. A sort group is usable if it has at least 12 non-sortal entries from at least 4 distinct source rules. 'person' will dominate; also expect student / animal / employee-like groups.\nIf fewer than 150 entries survive 2d, run 2a-2d on the MALLS fallback (c), up to 150 more rules.\n\nSTEP 3. COMPOSITION (about 40 min, $0; fully deterministic, seeded by sha1).\nWrite compose.py with 9 templates. N is the group noun, Ci are condition phrases (vp_sg_pos, or vp_sg_neg for at most 1 in 5 conditions), Q is the consequent phrase, E is the exception phrase and P is the proviso phrase. The FOL uses S(x) for the sortal atom, Ai for condition literals, Qx for the consequent literal, Ex for the exception and Px for the proviso.\n  T1 'Every N who C1, who C2, and who C3 Q, unless the N E.'\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> Qx)\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> ~Ex))\n  T2 'If a N C1 and C2, and the N also C3, then the N Q, except when the N E.' Readings as T1.\n  T3 'Any N that C1 and that C2 Q, provided that the N P and C3.'\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & Px & A3 -> Qx)\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 -> (Qx <-> (Px & A3)))\n  T4 'A N who C1 and who C2 Q as long as the N C3, unless the N E.'\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> Qx)\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> ~Ex))\n     A3 stays a plain condition, so the reading is single-exception.\n  T5 NESTED 'Every N who C1, provided that the N C2, Q unless the N E, and every such N also C3 ... ' is NOT used. It is replaced by 'Every N who C1 and who, provided that the N C2, also C3 Q unless the N E.' Readings as T1, with the nesting flag recorded.\n  T6 'Except when the N E, every N who C1 and who C2 Q, provided that the N C3.'\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> Qx)\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> ~Ex))\n  T7 'Anyone who C1, C2, and C3 Q unless they E.'\n     person group only; no sortal atom; 'they' takes vp phrases re-inflected to plural by a fixed is->are / has->have / does->do / -s stripping table, validated by spaCy.\n  T8 'If a N C1, C2, and C3, then the N Q, but only if the N P.'\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & Qx -> Px)   (the literal 'only if')\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> Px))\n     The converse forall x (... & Px -> Qx) is stored as reading_converse. It is NOT accepted: a candidate matching it gets the flag ONLY_IF_CONVERSE and goes to adjudication.\n  T9 'No N who C1, who C2, and who C3 Q, unless the N E.'\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> ~Qx)\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (~Qx <-> ~Ex))\n  At least 8 surface variants in total. Each template has 2 lexical variants (e.g. 'Every/Each', 'who/that', 'unless/except if'), chosen by sha1(sentence_seed).\n  Unit tests (tests/test_templates.py) check each template's weak and strong FOL on hand-written instances before any composition. The executor also reads 10 filled instances per template and records a PASS note in the card.\n\nComposition constraints:\n- conditions come from at least 2 distinct source rules; Q, E and P come from rules different from each other;\n- no predicate is used twice in a sentence;\n- each lexicon entry is used in at most 4 sentences;\n- each (sorted condition set, Q, E) combination is unique.\n\nFilters, applied in order, with each count logged:\n  words ≥25 -> nconds(weak) ≥3 -> at least 2 non-sortal restrictor atoms -> z3 non-trivial (weak neither valid nor unsat; weak not equivalent to strong; no VACUOUS atom) -> no screen hash collision -> no string duplicate.\nOver-generate about 450, then run the fluency rating.\n\nSTEP 4. FLUENCY and REFERENCE AUDIT (about 25 min, ≤$0.8).\n4a. Fluency: claude-haiku-4.5, 10 sentences per call, temperature 0. It rates grammaticality and fluency 1-5 and is explicitly told NOT to judge truth or plausibility. Drop ratings below 3. Store all ratings.\n4b. Pre-generation Sonnet audit of EVERY surviving sentence, 5 per call. The model sees the sentence and reading 1 / reading 2 unlabelled; it is not told they are verified. For each reading it answers FAITHFUL / UNFAITHFUL (under some legitimate reading) with ops. A sentence is REF_FLAGGED if a reading it considers the plain reading is judged UNFAITHFUL.\n  Log the flagged rate with a Wilson CI. Drop flagged sentences, and drop any lexicon entry implicated in 2 or more flags.\n  If the flagged rate exceeds 15%, stop and inspect the templates before continuing; any fix happens BEFORE generation and is logged.\n4c. Pick the final 250: sha1 order, stratified at least 20 per template, 'only if' at most 25 sentences, a spread over word bins 25-29 / 30-34 / ≥35.\n  The next-best unused sentences (all conditions exactly 3, templates T1/T2/T7, no nesting) form the TOP-UP RESERVE of 100. Their audit is done now as well.\n\nSTEP 5. PRE-REGISTRATION (5 min).\nWrite prereg_rcomp.json and record its sha256 in the card. It contains: templates and readings; filters; the label rule (STEP 7); routing and priority (STEP 8); caps; the testability rule; the top-up rule; and the perturbation spec (STEP 9).\nTOP-UP RULE: after STEP 8, if tiers A+B hold fewer than 50 CORRECT LLM rows or fewer than 25 CORRECT sentences (and likewise for ERROR), generate, label and adjudicate the 100-sentence reserve with the full slot set. Cap $1.3 for the top-up. It is flagged topup_batch and reported with and without the top-up.\n\nSTEP 6. CANDIDATE GENERATION (about 30 min, ≤$1.5).\nRun E's generate.py logic, unchanged, over rcomp sentences, keyed (sentence_id, slot, prompt_variant) in raw/generations.jsonl:\n- all 10 few-shot slots;\n- zero-shot for G1b and G2, so the 13 system x variant rows match E;\n- slot F (gpt-5.1, effort low, max_tokens 4000) on the sha1-first 80 sentences.\nPilot on 12 sentences first and measure $ per call per slot; extrapolate and assert the projection is ≤$1.5, otherwise drop F to 40 sentences.\nThe key-limit failure pattern (E's done_keys) is retried; other final failures are kept as UNPARSEABLE with api_error set.\nWrite rcomp_peers.json: {sentence_id: {text, reference_fol_weak, reference_fol_strong, template_id, candidates: [{item_id, system, family, slot, prompt_variant, raw_output, candidate_fol, parse_ok}]}}.\n\nSTEP 7. SOLVER LABELS (about 60 min CPU, $0).\nUse a ProcessPoolExecutor with 4 workers and a hard per-task timeout of 60 s; a pair cache keyed on sha1(cand|ref).\nFor each candidate c:\n  lw = auto_label(c, weak, repair=False); ls = auto_label(c, strong, repair=False) (skip ls if there is no strong reading); plus the converse check for T8.\n  - If lw or ls is CORRECT: CORRECT, tier A, matched_reading = weak/strong.\n  - Else if either is VOCAB_GRAN: VOCAB_GRAN, routed to adjudication.\n  - Else if either is TIMEOUT_UNKNOWN: routed.\n  - Else run minimal_typed_repair against weak AND strong (8 s each). ADJUDICATION (about 30 min, ≤$2.5 main; the known-label check ≤$0.2 runs first).\nPrompt: adjudication_prompt.txt, per the strategy's ADJ-PROMPT spec.\n- The model sees the ORIGINAL sentence, ONE candidate FOL, and the reference tagged VERIFIED.\n- For R_COMP both readings are shown: 'VERIFIED reference, reading 1 (weak exception)' and 'reading 2 (strong exception)', with the line 'a translation equivalent in meaning to either reading is faithful'. This is the only addition to the spec, and it is logged.\n- The prompt says predicate names and decomposition may legitimately differ, and that only meaning is judged.\n- It returns JSON {verdict: FAITHFUL|UNFAITHFUL|AMBIGUOUS_READING, ops ⊆ [NEG, REV, QUANT, RESTR, CONN, MOVE, DROP, ADD, SWAP, BIND, SCOPE, UNGLUE, MEANING_RENAME, OTHER], location, reference_wrong}.\n- Settings: temperature 0, max_tokens 200, no reasoning. Parse failures are retried once, then the row is UNRESOLVED.\n8a. KNOWN-LABEL CHECK: 30 PERTURB and 30 PERTURB_CONTROL rows on R_COMP bases (built in STEP 9 first; ops spread, DROP/ADD/MEANING_RENAME included), through the identical prompt. Report balanced accuracy. If it is below 0.80, all tier-B R_COMP labels are marked provisional in the card; do not stop.\n8b. Queue, in strict priority order and sentence-complete, each priority in sha1(sentence_id) order, so any cap cut leaves a random subset of sentences fully labelled:\n  P1 VOCAB_GRAN;\n  P2 COMPOUND and TIMEOUT_UNKNOWN;\n  P3 ERROR with addrop_only_suspect, a SORTAL/ARITY_REIFY/XOR_OR flag, or ONLY_IF_CONVERSE;\n  P4 a 20% sha1 sample of the other solver ERRORs (to estimate solver-ERROR precision; E's value was 0.705).\n  Estimated class-items: about 900 at about $0.0027 each.\nFinal label rule:\n  - VOCAB_GRAN/COMPOUND/TIMEOUT + FAITHFUL: CORRECT, tier B, correct_not_equivalent = true.\n  - The same + UNFAITHFUL: ERROR (adjudicator ops; MEANING_RENAME added for VOCAB_GRAN), tier B.\n  - Solver ERROR + FAITHFUL: CONTESTED.\n  - Solver ERROR + UNFAITHFUL: ERROR, tier A (confirmed).\n  - AMBIGUOUS_READING: reading_choice = true.\n  - Routed but not reached before the cap: UNRESOLVED for P1/P2. Expect about 4,500-6,000 rows; cap 6,000. Label ERROR, error_ops = [op].\nControls per base, each z3-verified equivalent (RENAME: equivalent under the known inverse map):\n  - RENAME: RENAME_SYN (WordNet synonym of a name token) when available, else RENAME_NONCE;\n  - REORDER: commute an and/or pair, or same-type quantifiers;\n  - CONTRAPOSITIVE: DEMORGAN if there is no implication.\n  Label CORRECT, metadata_fold PERTURB_CONTROL.\nPerturbation rows use system = 'PERTURB:<op>:<polarity>' or 'CONTROL:<type>' in the item_id recipe.\n\nSTEP 10. Synthetic perturbations are a separate, clearly marked diagnostic, because they do not match real error distributions (Goyal & Durrett; Just et al.).\n(2) Semi-synthetic control. Where natural data cannot supply trusted labels, the field accepts templated or prover-derived items with gold computed by construction (RuleTaker/ProofWriter, ProverQA, LogicBench). An adjudicator known-label check (60 rows) measures label quality on the long stratum itself. Everything is pre-registered in prereg_rcomp.json before generation. The testability of R_COMP is declared in the card before any metric runs. Hard stop $9.5, planned spend about $6.5.\",\n  \"runpod_compute_profile\": \"cpu_plus\",\n  \"target_num_datasets\": 2,\n  \"builds_on\": \"This plan builds directly on dataset E (art_U4Hsqt4Ay9Tg). The move is DEEPEN, so nothing starts fresh. The executor copies everything from E_DIR = /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_1/gen_art/gen_art_dataset_1 into its workspace; it never writes to E_DIR.\\n(a) The shared solver labeller, verbatim: E_DIR/labeller/fol.py (the parser with the xor-precedence fix; profile() gives per-predicate DOWN/UP monotonicity), repair_census.py (align, gran_bridge and the edits() typed-operator generator, reused here as the FORWARD mutant generator), label_lib.py (auto_label, equivalent_modulo_vocab, minimal_typed_repair, convention_flags), disguise.py (per-sentence CVCVC nonce bijection) and complexity_counts.py (nconds / nquant / depth, the counters behind E's strata).\\n(b) E_DIR/src/select_sentences.py: norm(), h(), sid(), build_exclusion() (the 2,054 screen exclusion hashes), ctrl_pool() (the FOLIO-v2-train x folio-refined agreement construction), exception_type() and TEXT_COND. Also E_DIR/src/normalise.py, which turns raw outputs into FOL. And E_DIR/src/or_client.py, the async OpenRouter client with a per-call cost ledger, phase caps and a BudgetExceeded stop.\\n(c) E_DIR/src/generate.py: the SLOTS table (exact model ids and extra params), MAX_TOK, ZERO_SHOT_SLOTS, and the resumable jsonl-append pattern. It also holds prompts/fewshot_v1.txt and zeroshot_v1.txt (frozen, sha1-checked).\\n(d) Data: E_DIR/full_data_out.json group heldout_sentences (700 rows with reference_status; source of the 131 GOLD_PANEL_OK + TRUSTED_AGREED references and up to 69 PANEL_REPAIRED ones), E_DIR/work/sentences.json, E_DIR/work/fewshot_exemplars.json (excluded as sources), E_DIR/data_local/yfxiao__folio-refined__train.csv and E_DIR/data_local/*MALLS*, and E_DIR/raw/hf/tasksource__folio/folio_v2_train.jsonl. E's card §6 and §3 findings are the reason for the routing rules. VOCAB_GRAN is NOT auto-CORRECT, because 58 of 59 solver-to-panel transitions were CORRECT->ERROR. Solver ERROR precision is only 0.705, so ADD/DROP-only and convention-flagged ERRORs are routed to adjudication. L25 tier A is untestable, and the panel rejects 85% of L25 references: that is why R_COMP exists at all.\\n(f) The iteration-2 strategy's shared ADJ-PROMPT spec (gen_strat_1 rationale). If the sibling R_ADJ dataset has already written adjudication_prompt.txt (glob /ai-inventor/aii_data/runs/run_u75jRHUss0zo/3_invention_loop/iter_2/gen_art/*/adjudication_prompt.txt) when this executor starts, that exact text and model are used. Otherwise the executor writes the prompt from the spec and records its sha1, so the two regimes can be joined.\\n(g) Negative results reused as design constraints. E's cheap panel (Haiku/GLM/Kimi) is strict: it accepts only 0.613 of expert-corrected formulas and rejects 82% of MALLS gold. That is the evidence for the model choice.\",\n  \"ideal_dataset_criteria\": \"R_COMP (metadata_fold='R_COMP').\\n(1) About 250 sentences, each with: at least 25 whitespace words; nconds(weak reference) at least 3, using E's complexity_counts.nconds, AND at least 2 non-sortal restrictor conditions; exactly one exception or proviso clause (unless / except when / except if / provided that / as long as / but only if). Nine fixed templates, each with at least 20 sentences; 'but only if' is capped at 10%. A sentence is kept only if its z3 checks pass: the weak reading is neither valid nor unsatisfiable, no atom is VACUOUS under fol.profile, and weak and strong readings are non-equivalent wherever a strong reading is defined.\\n(2) The reference is trusted by construction. Every atom's English phrase comes from an audited lexicon entry mined from a verified, screen-disjoint short reference. Every template's two readings are hand-verified in unit tests. Each composed sentence also passes a pre-generation Sonnet audit.\\n(3) Fluency is rated at least 3 of 5, and the full distribution is reported.\\n(4) Real candidates come from the same generator slots, prompt, temperature 0 and max_tokens as E: G1 llama-3.1-8b, G1b llama-3.3-70b, G2 qwen3-235b-a22b-2507, G3 mistral-small-3.2-24b, G4 deepseek-v3.2 (reasoning off), G5 gemma-3-27b, G6 phi-4, G7 gpt-4.1-mini, G8 gemini-2.5-flash (reasoning max_tokens 0), G9 command-r7b; also zero-shot G1b/G2, and F gpt-5.1 (effort low) on 80 sentences. Unparseable outputs are kept as UNPARSEABLE rows.\\n(5) Labels come from E's solver labeller against BOTH readings. A candidate equivalent to either reading is CORRECT. Adjudicator-decided rows carry the verdict, ops, location and reference_wrong.\\n(6) Testable means at least 50 CORRECT and at least 50 ERROR LLM rows, each spread over at least 25 sentences, in tiers A+B, excluding CONTESTED, reading_choice and REF_FLAGGED rows. This is declared before any metric runs.\\n(7) Provenance per sentence: template_id, surface_variant, sha1 seed, source rule ids and atom lexicon ids.\\n\\nPERTURB (metadata_fold 'PERTURB' / 'PERTURB_CONTROL').\\n300 bases: 200 tier-A-verified E references and 100 R_COMP weak references. Each base gets the 13 operators (NEG, REV, QUANT, RESTR, CONN, MOVE, DROP, ADD, SWAP, BIND, SCOPE, UNGLUE, MEANING_RENAME) at DOWN and at UP positions where they are applicable. Every mutant is z3-verified non-equivalent to the base and to every accepted reading; UNKNOWN mutants are discarded. Each base also gets 3 z3-verified meaning-preserving controls: RENAME (synonym or nonce subtype), REORDER, and CONTRAPOSITIVE (DEMORGAN if the base has no implication). Every row carries base_item_id, sentence_id, operator, subtype, position_polarity, polarity_method and matched_pair_id.\\n\\nFORMAT.\\nOne aii-json exp_sel_data_out file, full_data_out.json, with 4 groups: rcomp_candidates, rcomp_sentences, perturb_suite and adjudicator_check. The input field is a JSON string {text, candidate_fol, reference_fol (= the weak reading, for E-reader compatibility), reference_fol_weak, reference_fol_strong, system, prompt_variant}. The output field is CORRECT, ERROR, CONTESTED, UNRESOLVED or UNPARSEABLE. item_id = sha1(system + '|' + norm(text) + '|' + raw_output)[:16], E's exact recipe. Also provided: disguised_text and disguised_fol from E's disguise.py; rcomp_peers.json; lexicon.json; prereg_rcomp.json; adjudication_prompt.txt; dataset_card.md; cost_ledger.jsonl. Each file stays under 30 MB; split with aii-file-size-limit if needed.\",\n  \"dataset_search_plan\": \"No external dataset search is needed. Every source is fixed, already local or restorable, and licensed. Before planning any call sweep, the executor reads the skills aii-python, aii-parallel-computing, aii-long-running-tasks, aii-openrouter-llms and aii-json. All paths below are relative to the workspace; E_DIR is the iteration-1 dataset directory given in builds_on.\\n\\nSTEP 0. SETUP (about 20 min, $0).\\n- Copy E_DIR/labeller/, the E_DIR/src files {select_sentences.py, normalise.py, or_client.py, generate.py}, E_DIR/prompts/, E_DIR/tests/test_fol.py and E_DIR/pyproject.toml into the workspace (labeller/, src_e/, prompts/, tests/).\\n- Create a venv with uv; add spacy with en_core_web_sm, nltk wordnet, and z3-solver (already a dependency of E).\\n- Run pytest tests/test_fol.py. It must pass unchanged.\\n- Verify sha1(prompts/fewshot_v1.txt) equals the prompt_sha1 in E's raw/generations.jsonl records.\\n- Set AII_HARD_CAP=9.5 in or_client, and give each phase a cap (see the BUDGET table).\\n- Snapshot the OpenRouter model list and confirm that every slot id in generate.py SLOTS exists. If one 404s, use the nearest same-family successor and log the substitution in generation_manifest.json. Never change a family.\\n\\nSTEP 1. SOURCE ATOM POOL (about 40 min, $0).\\nBuild source_rules.json from, in priority order:\\n  (a) E heldout_sentences with reference_status TRUSTED_AGREED (36, FOLIO CTRL) or GOLD_PANEL_OK (95, MALLS);\\n  (b) ctrl_pool() output: FOLIO-v2-train premises with agreement_type in {IDENTICAL_STRING, EQ, VOCAB} (about 1,500), minus E's 6 few-shot exemplars;\\n  (c) FALLBACK, only if fewer than 150 audited lexicon entries survive STEP 2: MALLS-train sentences of 15 words or fewer, shaped as below, sha1 order.\\nApply the exclusion hashes from build_exclusion() to every source text; screen collisions must be 0.\\nFrom each source formula, extract only atoms over the rule's single universally quantified variable x:\\n- unary P(x), or binary P(x, const) / P(const, x) with a lowercase constant;\\n- no atoms with other bound variables.\\nRecord for each atom: role (restrictor, consequent or other), sortal flag, and the source sentence and formula.\\nPrefer rules shaped as forall x (L1 [and L2 [and L3]] -> L), where each L is a literal over x.\\n\\nSTEP 2. ATOM LEXICON: extraction, deterministic checks, audit (about 50 min, ≤$0.8).\\n2a. Extraction. anthropic/claude-haiku-4.5 (an Anthropic model; neither a generator nor a metric-judge family), 5 source rules per call, temperature 0. Input: the sentence, the formula and the atom list. Output JSON per atom:\\n  {vp_sg_pos (3rd-person-singular verb phrase for 'the person ___', e.g. 'is a student', 'lives in Paris', 'can make cookies'), vp_sg_neg ('is not a student'), noun (only for sortal atoms, e.g. 'student'), subject_sort (person / animal / object / place / organisation / other), or SKIP if no faithful phrase exists}.\\n  Cap about 320 source rules (≈64 calls).\\n2b. Deterministic checks, reject on failure:\\n  - every content-word Porter stem of vp_sg_pos appears in the source sentence stems, or is a WordNet synonym of one (auxiliaries, articles and 'not' are exempt);\\n  - the first token of vp_sg_pos is tagged VBZ or MD by spaCy, or is is/has/does;\\n  - vp_sg_neg contains not / n't / no / never;\\n  - the constant in a binary atom appears in the phrase (case-insensitive, camelCase split).\\n2c. Sonnet audit. The adjudicator model (default anthropic/claude-sonnet-4.6, or the sibling R_ADJ's gated model), 20 entries per call. It sees each (source sentence, formula, atom, vp_pos, vp_neg) and returns OK / WRONG plus a reason code.\\n2d. Keep the OK entries and write lexicon.json, with lexicon_id = sha1(atom|vp)[:10].\\n2e. Resolve name collisions across sources. If two entries share a predicate name but differ in arity or in phrase meaning, rename the later one to Name_k and record the change.\\n2f. Group entries by subject_sort. A sort group is usable if it has at least 12 non-sortal entries from at least 4 distinct source rules. 'person' will dominate; also expect student / animal / employee-like groups.\\nIf fewer than 150 entries survive 2d, run 2a-2d on the MALLS fallback (c), up to 150 more rules.\\n\\nSTEP 3. COMPOSITION (about 40 min, $0; fully deterministic, seeded by sha1).\\nWrite compose.py with 9 templates. N is the group noun, Ci are condition phrases (vp_sg_pos, or vp_sg_neg for at most 1 in 5 conditions), Q is the consequent phrase, E is the exception phrase and P is the proviso phrase. The FOL uses S(x) for the sortal atom, Ai for condition literals, Qx for the consequent literal, Ex for the exception and Px for the proviso.\\n  T1 'Every N who C1, who C2, and who C3 Q, unless the N E.'\\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> Qx)\\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> ~Ex))\\n  T2 'If a N C1 and C2, and the N also C3, then the N Q, except when the N E.' Readings as T1.\\n  T3 'Any N that C1 and that C2 Q, provided that the N P and C3.'\\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & Px & A3 -> Qx)\\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 -> (Qx <-> (Px & A3)))\\n  T4 'A N who C1 and who C2 Q as long as the N C3, unless the N E.'\\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> Qx)\\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> ~Ex))\\n     A3 stays a plain condition, so the reading is single-exception.\\n  T5 NESTED 'Every N who C1, provided that the N C2, Q unless the N E, and every such N also C3 ... ' is NOT used. It is replaced by 'Every N who C1 and who, provided that the N C2, also C3 Q unless the N E.' Readings as T1, with the nesting flag recorded.\\n  T6 'Except when the N E, every N who C1 and who C2 Q, provided that the N C3.'\\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> Qx)\\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> ~Ex))\\n  T7 'Anyone who C1, C2, and C3 Q unless they E.'\\n     person group only; no sortal atom; 'they' takes vp phrases re-inflected to plural by a fixed is->are / has->have / does->do / -s stripping table, validated by spaCy.\\n  T8 'If a N C1, C2, and C3, then the N Q, but only if the N P.'\\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & Qx -> Px)   (the literal 'only if')\\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (Qx <-> Px))\\n     The converse forall x (... & Px -> Qx) is stored as reading_converse. It is NOT accepted: a candidate matching it gets the flag ONLY_IF_CONVERSE and goes to adjudication.\\n  T9 'No N who C1, who C2, and who C3 Q, unless the N E.'\\n     weak:   forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 & ~Ex -> ~Qx)\\n     strong: forall x (S & A1 & A2 & A3 -> (~Qx <-> ~Ex))\\n  At least 8 surface variants in total. Each template has 2 lexical variants (e.g. 'Every/Each', 'who/that', 'unless/except if'), chosen by sha1(sentence_seed).\\n  Unit tests (tests/test_templates.py) check each template's weak and strong FOL on hand-written instances before any composition. The executor also reads 10 filled instances per template and records a PASS note in the card.\\n\\nComposition constraints:\\n- conditions come from at least 2 distinct source rules; Q, E and P come from rules different from each other;\\n- no predicate is used twice in a sentence;\\n- each lexicon entry is used in at most 4 sentences;\\n- each (sorted condition set, Q, E) combination is unique.\\n\\nFilters, applied in order, with each count logged:\\n  words ≥25 -> nconds(weak) ≥3 -> at least 2 non-sortal restrictor atoms -> z3 non-trivial (weak neither valid nor unsat; weak not equivalent to strong; no VACUOUS atom) -> no screen hash collision -> no string duplicate.\\nOver-generate about 450, then run the fluency rating.\\n\\nSTEP 4. FLUENCY and REFERENCE AUDIT (about 25 min, ≤$0.8).\\n4a. Fluency: claude-haiku-4.5, 10 sentences per call, temperature 0. It rates grammaticality and fluency 1-5 and is explicitly told NOT to judge truth or plausibility. Drop ratings below 3. Store all ratings.\\n4b. Pre-generation Sonnet audit of EVERY surviving sentence, 5 per call. The model sees the sentence and reading 1 / reading 2 unlabelled; it is not told they are verified. For each reading it answers FAITHFUL / UNFAITHFUL (under some legitimate reading) with ops. A sentence is REF_FLAGGED if a reading it considers the plain reading is judged UNFAITHFUL.\\n  Log the flagged rate with a Wilson CI. Drop flagged sentences, and drop any lexicon entry implicated in 2 or more flags.\\n  If the flagged rate exceeds 15%, stop and inspect the templates before continuing; any fix happens BEFORE generation and is logged.\\n4c. Pick the final 250: sha1 order, stratified at least 20 per template, 'only if' at most 25 sentences, a spread over word bins 25-29 / 30-34 / ≥35.\\n  The next-best unused sentences (all conditions exactly 3, templates T1/T2/T7, no nesting) form the TOP-UP RESERVE of 100. Their audit is done now as well.\\n\\nSTEP 5. PRE-REGISTRATION (5 min).\\nWrite prereg_rcomp.json and record its sha256 in the card. It contains: templates and readings; filters; the label rule (STEP 7); routing and priority (STEP 8); caps; the testability rule; the top-up rule; and the perturbation spec (STEP 9).\\nTOP-UP RULE: after STEP 8, if tiers A+B hold fewer than 50 CORRECT LLM rows or fewer than 25 CORRECT sentences (and likewise for ERROR), generate, label and adjudicate the 100-sentence reserve with the full slot set. Cap $1.3 for the top-up. It is flagged topup_batch and reported with and without the top-up.\\n\\nSTEP 6. CANDIDATE GENERATION (about 30 min, ≤$1.5).\\nRun E's generate.py logic, unchanged, over rcomp sentences, keyed (sentence_id, slot, prompt_variant) in raw/generations.jsonl:\\n- all 10 few-shot slots;\\n- zero-shot for G1b and G2, so the 13 system x variant rows match E;\\n- slot F (gpt-5.1, effort low, max_tokens 4000) on the sha1-first 80 sentences.\\nPilot on 12 sentences first and measure $ per call per slot; extrapolate and assert the projection is ≤$1.5, otherwise drop F to 40 sentences.\\nThe key-limit failure pattern (E's done_keys) is retried; other final failures are kept as UNPARSEABLE with api_error set.\\nWrite rcomp_peers.json: {sentence_id: {text, reference_fol_weak, reference_fol_strong, template_id, candidates: [{item_id, system, family, slot, prompt_variant, raw_output, candidate_fol, parse_ok}]}}.\\n\\nSTEP 7. SOLVER LABELS (about 60 min CPU, $0).\\nUse a ProcessPoolExecutor with 4 workers and a hard per-task timeout of 60 s; a pair cache keyed on sha1(cand|ref).\\nFor each candidate c:\\n  lw = auto_label(c, weak, repair=False); ls = auto_label(c, strong, repair=False) (skip ls if there is no strong reading); plus the converse check for T8.\\n  - If lw or ls is CORRECT: CORRECT, tier A, matched_reading = weak/strong.\\n  - Else if either is VOCAB_GRAN: VOCAB_GRAN, routed to adjudication.\\n  - Else if either is TIMEOUT_UNKNOWN: routed.\\n  - Else run minimal_typed_repair against weak AND strong (8 s each). Take the FOUND repair with the fewest ops (ties go to weak): ERROR(ops), tier A, with repair_reading. If neither is FOUND: COMPOUND, routed.\\n  - Parse failure: UNPARSEABLE, kept.\\nAlso store convention_flags(c, weak) and addrop_only_suspect (the repair is only ADD/DROP).\\nCollapse classes per sentence ONLY by identical normalised string or plain z3 EQ with identical symbols, never modulo vocabulary. The label then propagates within the class.\\nDiagnostic only, never a label: lex_anchored_vocab = every renamed predicate pair in align() shares a stem with the reference atom's lexicon phrase. Its agreement with the adjudicator is reported, and the card warns that it shares an instrument with L2-bow.\\n\\nSTEP 8. ADJUDICATION (about 30 min, ≤$2.5 main; the known-label check ≤$0.2 runs first).\\nPrompt: adjudication_prompt.txt, per the strategy's ADJ-PROMPT spec.\\n- The model sees the ORIGINAL sentence, ONE candidate FOL, and the reference tagged VERIFIED.\\n- For R_COMP both readings are shown: 'VERIFIED reference, reading 1 (weak exception)' and 'reading 2 (strong exception)', with the line 'a translation equivalent in meaning to either reading is faithful'. This is the only addition to the spec, and it is logged.\\n- The prompt says predicate names and decomposition may legitimately differ, and that only meaning is judged.\\n- It returns JSON {verdict: FAITHFUL|UNFAITHFUL|AMBIGUOUS_READING, ops ⊆ [NEG, REV, QUANT, RESTR, CONN, MOVE, DROP, ADD, SWAP, BIND, SCOPE, UNGLUE, MEANING_RENAME, OTHER], location, reference_wrong}.\\n- Settings: temperature 0, max_tokens 200, no reasoning. Parse failures are retried once, then the row is UNRESOLVED.\\n8a. KNOWN-LABEL CHECK: 30 PERTURB and 30 PERTURB_CONTROL rows on R_COMP bases (built in STEP 9 first; ops spread, DROP/ADD/MEANING_RENAME included), through the identical prompt. Report balanced accuracy. If it is below 0.80, all tier-B R_COMP labels are marked provisional in the card; do not stop.\\n8b. Queue, in strict priority order and sentence-complete, each priority in sha1(sentence_id) order, so any cap cut leaves a random subset of sentences fully labelled:\\n  P1 VOCAB_GRAN;\\n  P2 COMPOUND and TIMEOUT_UNKNOWN;\\n  P3 ERROR with addrop_only_suspect, a SORTAL/ARITY_REIFY/XOR_OR flag, or ONLY_IF_CONVERSE;\\n  P4 a 20% sha1 sample of the other solver ERRORs (to estimate solver-ERROR precision; E's value was 0.705).\\n  Estimated class-items: about 900 at about $0.0027 each.\\nFinal label rule:\\n  - VOCAB_GRAN/COMPOUND/TIMEOUT + FAITHFUL: CORRECT, tier B, correct_not_equivalent = true.\\n  - The same + UNFAITHFUL: ERROR (adjudicator ops; MEANING_RENAME added for VOCAB_GRAN), tier B.\\n  - Solver ERROR + FAITHFUL: CONTESTED.\\n  - Solver ERROR + UNFAITHFUL: ERROR, tier A (confirmed).\\n  - AMBIGUOUS_READING: reading_choice = true.\\n  - Routed but not reached before the cap: UNRESOLVED for P1/P2. For P3/P4 the solver ERROR stays, with adj_pending = true.\\n  - A sentence where 2 or more adjudications say reference_wrong = true: REF_FLAGGED, all its rows go to tier C and out of the primary pool.\\n\\nSTEP 9. TYPED-PERTURBATION SUITE (about 45 min CPU, $0).\\nBases, sha1 order:\\n- all E heldout_sentences with GOLD_PANEL_OK or TRUSTED_AGREED (131);\\n- then PANEL_REPAIRED, until 200 (base_trust = panel_repaired);\\n- then the first 100 R_COMP sentences (weak reading as base; mutants must be non-equivalent to weak, strong AND converse).\\nFor each base, generate mutants with repair_census.edits(base, donor), where donor is a sha1-chosen base from a different source story. It gives ADD_FOREIGN atoms and BIND constants.\\nAdd two own small operators:\\n  - ADD_INTERNAL: conjoin an existing atom of the base at a new restrictor or consequent position;\\n  - MEANING_RENAME: replace ALL occurrences of one predicate with a same-arity donor predicate whose name tokens are not WordNet synonyms.\\nposition_polarity:\\n  - atom-level edits: fol.profile(base)[pred] (UP / DOWN / NONMONO);\\n  - structural edits: the syntactic polarity of the edited node path (flipped by ~ and by an implication antecedent; <-> gives NONMONO).\\n  - polarity_method is recorded.\\nFor each (base, op, polarity in {DOWN, UP}), keep the first valid mutant in sha1(string) order. Valid means: emit->parse round-trips; z3 non-equivalent to the base and to every accepted reading at 3 s (UNKNOWN is discarded); non-trivial if the base is; not a duplicate.\\nmatched_pair_id links the DOWN and UP mutants of the same base x op. Expect about 4,500-6,000 rows; cap 6,000. Label ERROR, error_ops = [op].\\nControls per base, each z3-verified equivalent (RENAME: equivalent under the known inverse map):\\n  - RENAME: RENAME_SYN (WordNet synonym of a name token) when available, else RENAME_NONCE;\\n  - REORDER: commute an and/or pair, or same-type quantifiers;\\n  - CONTRAPOSITIVE: DEMORGAN if there is no implication.\\n  Label CORRECT, metadata_fold PERTURB_CONTROL.\\nPerturbation rows use system = 'PERTURB:<op>:<polarity>' or 'CONTROL:<type>' in the item_id recipe.\\n\\nSTEP 10. DISGUISE (about 15 min, $0).\\nUse E's disguise.py per sentence, with a bijection built over the text and ALL formulas of that sentence: references, candidates and mutants of the same base. Guard: plain-z3 equivalence of formula pairs before vs after disguise on 30 sentences; there must be 0 mismatches.\\n\\nSTEP 11. ASSEMBLE, CARD, VALIDATE (about 45 min).\\ndata.py builds full_data_out.json with the groups rcomp_candidates, rcomp_sentences (text, both readings, reading_converse, template_id, surface_variant, source_rule_ids, lexicon_ids, fluency, audit verdicts, ref_flagged, topup_batch), perturb_suite and adjudicator_check.\\nMetadata per candidate row: item_id, sentence_id, system, slot, family, prompt_variant, raw_output, parse_ok, auto_label_weak, auto_label_strong, matched_reading, repair_ops, repair_reading, repair_status, convention_flags, addrop_only_suspect, lex_anchored_vocab, adj_verdict, adj_ops, adj_location, adj_reference_wrong, label_tier, correct_not_equivalent, reading_choice, class_id, strata {words, n_quant, depth, n_conditions, exception_type (E's function), template_id, nested, word_bin}, disguised_text, disguised_fol and cost_usd.\\nValidate with aii-json (exp_sel_data_out), then make the mini and preview variants.\\ndataset_card.md must contain:\\n  - label counts per template x exception type x tier;\\n  - weak-vs-strong acceptance (the share of CORRECT matching only weak, only strong, or both);\\n  - the ONLY_IF_CONVERSE rate;\\n  - the fluency distribution;\\n  - the lexicon and reference audit rates with Wilson CIs;\\n  - the TESTABILITY DECLARATION (A+B and A-only), written BEFORE any metric runs and copied into prereg_rcomp.json under 'testability_declaration';\\n  - solver-vs-adjudicator confusion, with solver-ERROR precision from P4;\\n  - known-label check accuracy;\\n  - error rate per system on R_COMP vs E's L25 (is R_COMP easier?);\\n  - the perturbation counts per op x polarity, with DOWN/UP matched-pair coverage;\\n  - costs per phase;\\n  - licences: FOLIO CC-BY-SA-4.0, folio-refined MIT, MALLS CC-BY-NC-4.0; generator outputs follow provider terms;\\n  - known biases, including: templated English is cleaner than natural text; the Anthropic family is used for lexicon, audit and adjudication; the audit filter could favour Sonnet-legible items.\\nWrite README.md and .aii/manifest.yaml. Never tune anything on labels. Never write outside the workspace.\",\n  \"domain_practice\": \"What I read: the neurosymbolic handbook (hard rule: never score on FOLIO/MALLS gold as shipped, 36-39% wrong per 2606.02837; gold-free round-trip is an occupied lane; template sets trade realism for control); dataset E's card and README, code (label_lib, repair_census.edits, fol.profile, generate.py, select_sentences.ctrl_pool, complexity_counts) and its measured panel accuracies; and the iteration-2 strategy's ADJ-PROMPT spec and regime definitions. The following are cited from standing knowledge: FRANK (Pagnoni et al. 2021), Goyal & Durrett 2021, TRUE (Honovich et al. 2022), WMT MQM-vs-DA practice, Deutsch et al. 2021 (bootstrap CIs), mutation testing (Just et al. FSE 2014; Papadakis et al. ICSE 2018), RuleTaker/ProofWriter and ProverQA (prover-derived gold for templated logic), LogicBench (Parmar et al. ACL 2024, templated non-monotonic/exception patterns) and Datasheets for Datasets (Gebru et al.).\\n\\nHOW A META-EVALUATION SET OF THIS KIND IS BUILT.\\n(1) Labels. Metric meta-evaluation is only believed on REAL system outputs with labels whose source is named. Gold is audited, and correct-but-not-equivalent outputs are handled explicitly: FRANK and TRUE annotate real summaries, and WMT uses MQM. Synthetic perturbations are a separate, clearly marked diagnostic, because they do not match real error distributions (Goyal & Durrett; Just et al.).\\n(2) Semi-synthetic control. Where natural data cannot supply trusted labels, the field accepts templated or prover-derived items with gold computed by construction (RuleTaker/ProofWriter, ProverQA, LogicBench). The known cost is surface regularity and shortcut risk. They are therefore reported as a separate stratum and never pooled with natural data.\\n(3) Label quality statistics. Expected alongside the labels: adjudicator accuracy on known-label items, inter-rater or solver-vs-adjudicator confusion, the reference error rate with CIs, and the correct-but-not-equivalent rate.\\n(4) Size. Meta-evaluation strata are trusted at about 50 or more items per class, with bootstrap clustered by source sentence (E's own testability rule: at least 50/50 over at least 25 sentences). A paired ΔAUROC of 0.07 needs roughly 100+ per class. With ~250 sentences x ~12 LLM rows, R_COMP should give several hundred rows per class, over ~150+ sentences per class.\\n(5) Controls. Generator prompts, temperature and systems are held fixed across strata so that stratum differences are not prompt differences. Screen and confirmation sets are disjoint by normalised-text hash. Perturbation suites match operator positions (monotonicity, DOWN vs UP) so that per-type sensitivity is not confounded with polarity (SyGNS / monotonicity literature). Meaning-preserving rewrites are verified equivalent by solver, not assumed.\\n(6) Documentation. A datasheet-style card: provenance per item, licences, costs, known biases, and a testability declaration written before any metric is run.\",\n  \"practice_alignment\": \"MEETS:\\n(a) The candidates are real outputs of 13 system x variant rows over 10 families, with prompt, temperature and max_tokens identical to E, so R_COMP differs from E only in sentence construction.\\n(b) The reference is trusted by construction (an atom-level audited lexicon plus unit-tested template semantics) and then audited in full by a family-disjoint model before generation. Label source, tiers and the adjudicator's known-label accuracy are all reported.\\n(c) Correct-but-not-equivalent is handled. VOCAB_GRAN is never auto-CORRECT; both exception readings are accepted; the rejected only-if converse is flagged and adjudicated instead of silently counted as an error; SORTAL, ADD/DROP-only and other convention cases are routed.\\n(d) Screen disjointness is enforced by E's exclusion hashes.\\n(e) The testability declaration is frozen before any metric runs, and the top-up rule is pre-registered before generation.\\n(f) The perturbations are z3-verified against every accepted reading, with matched DOWN/UP positions and verified-equivalent controls. They are marked as a diagnostic fold, never pooled with real errors.\\n(g) Unparseable outputs are kept.\\n(h) A datasheet card with provenance, licences, costs and biases is delivered.\\n\\nDEPARTS:\\n(1) R_COMP sentences are semi-synthetic templated English, not natural text. This is justified because natural long, conditioned sentences with trustworthy references do not exist at this scale: E's L25 tier A is untestable, and its references are rejected at 85%. The cost is that R_COMP may be easier or more regular than real text. Mitigations: the card reports per-system error rates against E's L25; the stratum is never pooled; and conclusions from R_COMP are stated as 'reference-trusted long stratum', not as natural-text evidence.\\n(2) Most R_COMP CORRECT labels will be tier B (adjudicator-decided). Plain z3 EQ needs the reference's own predicate names, so tier A CORRECT will be scarce. So R_COMP is 'reference-trusted + Sonnet-adjudicated', not solver-only. The cost is that R_COMP and R_ADJ share an adjudicator family, so they are not independent regimes for CORRECT rows. The in-stratum known-label check and the solver-vs-adjudicator confusion quantify this, and the card states it. The deterministic lex_anchored_vocab column is kept as a diagnostic only, because it would share an instrument with L2-bow.\\n(3) One Anthropic family does lexicon extraction (Haiku), audit and adjudication (Sonnet). It is disjoint from all generators and all metric judges (Gemini/OpenAI/Qwen). The audit filter may favour Sonnet-legible sentences; this is disclosed.\\n(4) The adjudicator is given both readings in one prompt. That is a small format extension of the shared ADJ-PROMPT, logged and sha1-recorded. The same wording is used for the known-label check, so its accuracy applies.\\n(5) The adjudication budget ($2.5) may not cover all routed items. Truncation is sentence-complete in sha1 order, so the labelled subset is random at the sentence level, and the UNRESOLVED count is reported.\\n(6) Perturbation ADD and MEANING_RENAME partly use foreign-vocabulary atoms, which are easier for lexical metrics. The ADD_INTERNAL subtype and subtype labels let iteration 3 separate them.\\n(7) There is one prompt per generator at temperature 0, so system-level rankings on R_COMP are descriptive only, as in E.\"\n}\n", "structuredPatch": [], "originalFile": null, "userModified": false}
